*Hao Kong,Di Liu,Shuo Huai,Xiangzhong Luo,Weichen Liu,Ravi Subramaniam,Christian Makaya,Qian Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A framework (Smart Scissor) reduces CNN computation by two means: dynamically cropping foreground objects to preserve informative content at low resolution, and a compound shrinking strategy that jointly reduces depth, width, and resolution. The approach integrates these into a unified compression method, showing notable efficiency gains with modest accuracy improvements on ImageNet-1K (e.g., ResNet50: -41.5% FLOPs, +0.3% top-1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce spatial redundancy in images and structural redundancy in CNNs to achieve higher efficiency on resource-constrained devices while preserving accuracy.

Method: 1) A lightweight foreground predictor enables instance-aware dynamic image cropping to keep foreground objects at small resolutions; 2) A compound shrinking strategy compresses depth (channels), width (spatial), and resolution; 3) Combine both into the Smart Scissor framework.

Result: On ImageNet-1K, ResNet50 computation is reduced by 41.5% with a 0.3 percentage point gain in top-1 accuracy. Outperforms HRank (a CNN compression baseline) by 4.1 percentage points in top-1 accuracy at the same computational budget.

Conclusion: Dynamic foreground-aware cropping plus multi-dimensional CNN shrinking can significantly reduce computational cost while maintaining or slightly improving accuracy; the method is released with code and data.

Abstract: Scaling down the resolution of input images can greatly reduce the computational overhead of convolutional neural networks (CNNs), which is promising for edge AI. However, as an image usually contains much spatial redundancy, e.g., background pixels, directly shrinking the whole image will lose important features of the foreground object and lead to severe accuracy degradation. In this paper, we propose a dynamic image cropping framework to reduce the spatial redundancy by accurately cropping the foreground object from images. To achieve the instance-aware fine cropping, we introduce a lightweight foreground predictor to efficiently localize and crop the foreground of an image. The finely cropped images can be correctly recognized even at a small resolution. Meanwhile, computational redundancy also exists in CNN architectures. To pursue higher execution efficiency on resource-constrained embedded devices, we also propose a compound shrinking strategy to coordinately compress the three dimensions (depth, width, resolution) of CNNs. Eventually, we seamlessly combine the proposed dynamic image cropping and compound shrinking into a unified compression framework, Smart Scissor, which is expected to significantly reduce the computational overhead of CNNs while still maintaining high accuracy. Experiments on ImageNet-1K demonstrate that our method reduces the computational cost of ResNet50 by 41.5% while improving the top-1 accuracy by 0.3%. Moreover, compared to HRank, the state-of-the-art CNN compression framework, our method achieves 4.1% higher top-1 accuracy at the same computational cost. The codes and data are available at https://github.com/ntuliuteam/smart-scissor

</details>


### [26] [LoCA: Spatially-Aware Low-Rank Convolutional Adaptation of Vision Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06918)
*Sojung An,Junha Lee,Sujeong You,Nam Ik Cho,Donghyun Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LoCA is a convolution-aware PEFT method for vision foundation models that decouples channel and spatial adaptation to preserve spatial priors, using low-rank channel adaptation and SVD-refined spatial bases, achieving strong results without full fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Full fine-tuning of VFMs is costly and prone to catastrophic forgetting. Traditional LoRA applies 2D matrix-based adapters unsuitable for 4D convolutional kernels, which break spatial topology. A convolution-aware PEFT is needed.

Method: LoCA introduces a low-rank channel adaptation for cross-channel mixing and refines spatial bases from pre-trained kernels via Singular Value Decomposition (SVD), effectively decoupling channel and spatial adaptation within a PEFT framework.

Result: Empirical results show LoCA preserves pre-trained spatial priors and achieves competitive to state-of-the-art performance across fine-grained classification, domain-generalized semantic segmentation, and generative benchmarks.

Conclusion: LoCA offers a convolution-aware PEFT approach that respects spatial-channel structure in CNNs, enabling effective parameter-efficient adaptation of vision foundation models across diverse tasks.

Abstract: Pre-trained Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) provide strong visual representations for diverse downstream tasks. The key challenge of VFM adaptation stems from the prohibitive costs of full fine-tuning and catastrophic forgetting. To address this, Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) has emerged as the prevailing paradigm for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT). However, LoRA is typically designed for transformer self-attention layers parameterized by 2D matrices. Since convolutional kernels inherently couple spatial and channel information within a 4D tensor, forcing them into a monolithic 2D matrix disrupts the inherent spatial topology. In this paper, we propose Low-Rank Convolutional Adaptation (LoCA), a convolution-aware PEFT framework that addresses spatial-channel entanglement by decoupling channel and spatial adaptation. LoCA introduces a low-rank channel adaptation for dense cross-channel mixing and refines spatial bases extracted from pre-trained kernels via Singular Value Decomposition (SVD). Experimental results show that LoCA preserves pre-trained spatial priors and achieves competitive or state-of-the-art performance across fine-grained classification, domain-generalized semantic segmentation, and generative benchmarks.

</details>


### [27] [Compass: Prostate Cancer Detection Needs Multi-View Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06919)
*Paul F. R. Wilson,Mohamed Harmanani,Zhuoxin Guo,Obed K. Dzikunu,Hannes Cash,Adam Kinnaird,Brian Wodlinger,Purang Abolmaesumi,Parvin Mousavi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Compass introduces a transformer-based framework that aggregates evidence across a full μUS rotational study, modeling it as a stream of 2D frames, to predict frame- and study-level prostate cancer risk, outperforming single-frame baselines on multi-center data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clinical μUS analysis relies on 3D context from continuous rotational scans; single-frame AI methods miss cross-frame cues. The work aims to emulate expert workflows by leveraging study-wide information to improve detection accuracy and clinical decision support.

Method: Treat the μUS study as a sequence of 2D images. Fuse rotational sweep videos with biopsy-frame μUS frames. Use a transformer conditioned on the probe's rotational angle to aggregate evidence across the study. A decoder head outputs frame-level and study-level risk scores. Trained and evaluated on a multi-center clinical trial dataset with continuous rotational scans and biopsy videos; compared against baseline AI methods and expert risk scores; code released.

Result: Reported strong performance relative to baselines and expert risk scores, highlighting the benefit of multi-view context for μUS PCa detection and potential to complement human expertise.

Conclusion: Modeling μUS studies as multi-view streams with angle-conditioned transformer-based aggregation improves PCa detection over single-frame analysis and supports integration of AI into μUS-based diagnosis; code availability facilitates reproducibility.

Abstract: Artificial intelligence (AI) analysis of micro-ultrasound ($μ$US) has shown promise for prostate cancer (PCa) detection. However, most existing AI methods focus on the analysis of single $μ$US images in isolation. By contrast, expert $μ$US readers typically assess a full recorded video study, which provides three-dimensional context, to improve PCa detection compared to single-frame analysis. Inspired by this clinical workflow, we propose Compass, a novel AI methodology which models a $μ$US study as a stream of 2D images. Compass jointly integrates rotational sweep videos of the prostate with $μ$US frames acquired at the moment of biopsy, and performs evidence aggregation across the study using a transformer conditioned on the probe's rotational angle. Finally, a decoder head predicts frame-level and study-level risk scores for the patient. The model is trained and evaluated using a multi-center clinical trial dataset of $μ$US studies, including continuous rotational scans of the prostate and videos captured during biopsy acquisition. We compare the proposed method to baseline AI methods from the literature and to risk scores provided by clinical experts. Our framework shows strong performance, highlighting the value of multi-view context for $μ$US PCa detection, and providing a potentially powerful tool to complement human expertise in $μ$US-based PCa diagnosis. Our code is available at: https://github.com/mharmanani/Compass.

</details>


### [28] [Bi-PT: Bidirectional Cross-Attention Point Transformers for Four-Chamber Heart Reconstruction from Sparse Cardiac MRI Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06923)
*Chenchuhui Hu,Shaoming Pan,Leon Axel,Meng Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Bi-PT reconstructs 3D four-chamber heart meshes from sparse clinical CMR data using a NODE-based atlas-to-SPC deformation guided by bidirectional cross-attention and per-point semantic labels, yielding locally affine–diffeomorphic deformation and improved correspondences, achieving robust accuracy over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clinical CMR protocols provide sparse 2D views leading to error-prone 3D heart reconstructions; there is a need for a robust method that preserves topology and improves point correspondences.

Method: Use an atlas and a sparse point cloud (SPC) with bidirectional point cross-attention to learn robust per-point features; deform the atlas toward the target via a Neural Ordinary Differential Equation parameterized by per-point affine transformations and translations; incorporate per-point semantic labels; integrate a semantic label loss into Chamfer distance; add a smoothness regularization; enforce locally affine diffeomorphic deformation.

Result: Experiments show Bi-PT achieves accurate and robust performance compared to baselines (improved correspondence, stability under sparse data).

Conclusion: Bi-PT provides an effective pipeline for 3D four-chamber heart reconstruction from sparse CMR data with topology-preserving deformation and improved correspondences, outperforming baselines.

Abstract: We propose Bi-PT, a pipeline for reconstructing 3D four-chamber human heart meshes from clinical sparsely sampled cardiac magnetic resonance imaging (CMR) data. This work addresses the error-prone generation of 3D cardiac shape from a sparse point cloud (SPC) extracted from 2D long-axis and short-axis views used in routine clinical CMR protocols. Bi-PT enables accurate inference of the four-chamber heart mesh from the SPC by learning robust point features via bidirectional point cross-attention between an atlas and the SPC, together with per-point semantic labels that improve correspondence estimation. We formulate the deformation field as a Neural Ordinary Differential Equation (NODE) parameterized by a per-point affine transformation and translation to deform the atlas toward the target heart shape. By learning such a NODE, we can guarantee the deformation field to be a locally affine diffeomorphic deformation. We also integrate a semantic label loss into the Chamfer distance to encourage label-consistent correspondences and add a smoothness regularization to stabilize and improve the learning of the deformation field. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Bi-PT achieves accurate and robust performance compared to baselines.

</details>


### [29] [General Incomplete Multimodal Learning via Dynamic Quality Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06943)
*Xiangyu Meng,Shicai Wei*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GIML is a unified multimodal learning framework that handles both inter-modality missing and intra-modality degradation via dynamic quality perception, using a Noise-aware Quality Estimator and a Noise-Semantic Decoupled module to improve robustness and generalization; code is released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In real-world multimodal data, modalities can be entirely missing (inter-modality missing) or present but severely corrupted (intra-modality degradation). Existing methods typically address only one type, leaving a gap when both occur.

Method: GIML treats missing patterns as continuous information degradation and uses degradation-aware adaptive fusion. It introduces a Noise-aware Quality Estimator that maps corrupted features to noise intensity via controlled noise injection, and a Noise-Semantic Decoupled module that separates semantic content from noise interference.

Result: Extensive experiments across diverse modality datasets demonstrate the method’s effectiveness and generality, with code available for reproducibility.

Conclusion: GIML offers a unified approach to handle both missing modalities and degradation, improving robustness and generalization to unseen corruptions; it broadens applicability of multimodal learning in realistic settings.

Abstract: Multimodal learning robust to missing modalities is essential for real-world applications. Existing methods mainly focus on inter-modality missing, where entire modalities are absent, while overlooking intra-modality degradation, where modalities are present but severely corrupted. In practice, these two types of missing often coexist, making existing approaches ineffective. To address this limitation, we propose General Incomplete Multimodal Learning (GIML), a unified framework that simultaneously handles both inter-modality missing and intra-modality degradation through dynamic quality perception. Specifically, GIML models heterogeneous missing patterns as continuous modality information degradation, enabling degradation-aware adaptive fusion. To achieve reliable quality perception, we introduce a Noise-aware Quality Estimator that learns the mapping from corrupted features to noise intensity through controlled noise injection. Furthermore, we propose a Noise-Semantic Decoupled module that separates semantic information from noise interference. This improves robustness and generalization to unseen corruption patterns. Extensive experiments across datasets with diverse modality types demonstrate the effectiveness and generality of GIML. Code is available at: https://github.com/Yu-Five/GIML.

</details>


### [30] [Self-Supervised Pretraining Improves Cross-Site and Cross-Scale Robustness of Point Cloud Leaf-Wood Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06948)
*Heeju Mun,Tackang Yang,Yunsoo Nam,Changhyun Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SSL pretraining on point clouds with Point-M2AE improves leaf-wood segmentation across species and scales, yielding higher IoU and better downstream wood-volume estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Leaf-wood segmentation performance varies across forest types and sites. Self-supervised learning (SSL) can improve generalization in forestry point-cloud tasks, and its applicability to leaf-wood segmentation is untested.

Method: Pretrain Point-M2AE on ShapeNet-55 augmented with 2,400 individual tree point clouds; fine-tune and infer using recursive voxel subdivision to handle wide variation in point density; evaluate cross-country benchmark (four countries, three climates) against LeWos, CWLS, PointTransformer; conduct downstream wood-volume estimation with a quantitative structure model on 28 trees from Guyana, Indonesia, and Peru.

Result: IoU improved from 60.5% to 70.0% for needleleaf and from 69.7% to 76.3% for broadleaf; cross-site variation minimized; plot-level mIoU 84.7% (broadleaf) and 77.7% (needleleaf); MAE for wood volume 2.40 m^3, outperforming LeWos (5.94 m^3) and CWLS (5.27 m^3).

Conclusion: SSL pretraining with this approach generalizes leaf-wood segmentation across scales and sites and translates to more accurate downstream wood-volume estimates in tropical forests, without additional fine-tuning.

Abstract: The accuracy of existing leaf-wood segmentation methods for tree point clouds varies across forest types and sites. Self-supervised learning (SSL) on point clouds has improved the generalization of deep learning models for forestry point cloud tasks, including biomass regression and individual tree segmentation, but its applicability to leaf-wood segmentation remains untested. In this study, we pretrained Point-M2AE, a widely used SSL architecture for point clouds, on ShapeNet-55 augmented with 2,400 individual tree point clouds. For fine-tuning and inference, we used recursive voxel subdivision to handle the wide variation in point density across inputs, allowing the same model to operate at both individual-tree and plot scales without architecture change. Compared to the model without pretraining, the pretrained model improved wood IoU from 60.5% to 70.0% for needleleaf and from 69.7% to 76.3% for broadleaf trees. On a benchmark spanning four countries across three climatic zones, the pretrained model achieved the smallest cross-site variation and highest overall performance among compared methods (LeWos, CWLS, and PointTransformer). Plot-level segmentation maintained accuracy comparable to individual-tree performance, with mIoU of 84.7% for broadleaf and 77.7% for needleleaf plots, showing that the model generalizes across scales without additional finetuning. As a downstream test in tropical forests, where dense canopies make segmentation challenging, we applied our model and a quantitative structure model to estimate wood volume for 28 trees from Guyana, Indonesia, and Peru to assess whether the segmentation improvements from SSL pretraining translate into improved downstream performance. The resulting volume estimates achieved the lowest error among all methods tested (MAE = 2.40 m$^3$), less than half that of algorithmic baselines (LeWos: 5.94 m$^3$; CWLS: 5.27 m$^3$).

</details>


### [31] [SpiS-GAN: Spiral-Modulated Handwriting Synthesis with Star Operation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06949)
*Nguyen Duy Hieu,Dang Hoai Nam,Pham Hoang Giap,Quang Huu Hieu,Vo Nguyen Le Duy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SpiS-GAN: a spiral-modulated GAN for high-resolution, writer-style handwriting synthesis with Sobel edge loss to boost HTR training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address data scarcity for robust handwriting recognition (HTR) and overcome core limitations of prior synthetic handwriting models (fixed-grid receptive fields, aggressive downsampling losing structure, limited feature interactions, and lack of explicit edge constraints). Demonstrate cross-language style preservation (English and Vietnamese).

Method: Propose Star-Spiral Blocks with Modulated Elliptical SpiralFC and a star operation to capture complex stroke trajectories; introduce Spiral-Modulated discriminator for multi-domain flaw detection; add Sobel-Regularized Edge Reconstruction Loss to guide edge fidelity; evaluate on English and Vietnamese datasets.

Result: SpiS-GAN generates highly authentic handwriting images that preserve writer style across languages and lower downstream HTR error rates, outperforming current state-of-the-art synthetic methods.

Conclusion: The SpirAL-based architecture with edge-guided loss provides effective, high-resolution handwriting synthesis that improves realism and downstream HTR performance, indicating strong potential for data augmentation in multilingual handwriting tasks.

Abstract: Training robust handwriting recognition (HTR) systems requires massive amounts of annotated data, which is often difficult to acquire. While synthetic handwriting generation offers a practical solution to expand training sets, existing models struggle with several core issues. First, previous approaches, even MLP-based models fail to effectively trace cursive handwriting due to fixed-grid spatial receptive field. Second, their CNN-relied discriminators usually lose structural details through aggressive downsampling, making broken connections difficult to detect. Third, existing architectures are either limited to linear feature interactions or too expensive for high-resolution synthesis. Finally, existing approaches lack explicit edge constraints, often resulting in blurred stroke boundaries. To address these challenges, this study proposes a Spiral-Modulated Handwriting Synthesis framework based on Generative Adversarial Networks (SpiS-GAN). Our generator employs Star-Spiral Blocks combining proposed Modulated Elliptical SpiralFC with the star operation to capture spatial relationships and efficiently follow complex handwriting stroke trajectories, while a Spiral-Modulated discriminator is introduced for multi-domain flaws detection. Additionally, we introduce a Sobel-Regularized Edge Reconstruction Loss that provides edge guidance, ensuring every character remains clear and legible. Evaluations on the English and Vietnamese datasets demonstrate that SpiS-GAN significantly outperforms current state-of-the-art models. The generated images are highly authentic, accurately preserve the original writer's style across languages, and successfully lower error rates when training downstream HTR systems.

</details>


### [32] [HPR-SAM: Hierarchical Probabilistic Representation Learning for Prompt-free SAM-based Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06972)
*Yingzhen Hu,Yiheng Zhong,Keying Zhu,Zimu Zhang,Zihan Ye,Sifan Song,Jionglong Su,Xiaofeng Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes Hierarchical Probabilistic Representation (HPR) for prompt-free SAM-based medical image segmentation via Distributional Anatomical Representation (DAR), Multi-component Anatomical Representation (MAR), and Local Reliability Representation (LRR) with Hierarchical Prediction Fusion (HPF), achieving state-of-the-art results on Synapse and strong few-shot performance on LA and PROMISE12; compatible with SAM decoder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prompt-free SAM adaptation is limited by fixed anatomical representations; deterministic prototypes/semantic tokens fail to jointly capture global priors, intra-structure diversity, and local reliability; need a probabilistic, hierarchical representation to better model anatomy.

Method: Construct HPR with three complementary representations (DAR, MAR, LRR), fuse them via HPF, and retain compatibility with the original SAM decoder; train/validate on Synapse, LA, PROMISE12; provide code.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art on Synapse; best few-shot performance on LA and PROMISE12; demonstrates effectiveness of hierarchical probabilistic representation learning for prompt-free medical image segmentation.

Conclusion: Validates HPR-SAM as an effective, prompt-free segmentation framework; scalable and compatible with SAM; demonstrates improved medical image segmentation with hierarchical probabilistic representations.

Abstract: Prompt-free adaptation of the Segment Anything Model (SAM) has emerged as a promising paradigm for automatic medical image segmentation. Existing methods mainly focus on prompt generation, while overlooking that prompt quality is fundamentally constrained by the expressiveness of anatomical representations. However, deterministic prototypes or semantic tokens are insufficient to jointly capture global anatomical priors, intra-structure diversity, and local structural reliability. To address this limitation, we propose the Hierarchical Probabilistic Representation (HPR) framework, which learns complementary anatomical representations through Distributional Anatomical Representation (DAR), Multi-component Anatomical Representation (MAR), and Local Reliability Representation (LRR), and integrates their predictions via Hierarchical Prediction Fusion (HPF) while remaining compatible with the original SAM decoder. Experiments on the Synapse, LA, and PROMISE12 datasets demonstrate that HPR-SAM achieves state-of-the-art performance on Synapse and the best performance under few-shot settings on LA and PROMISE12, validating the effectiveness of the proposed hierarchical probabilistic representation learning framework for prompt-free medical image segmentation. Code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/HPR-SAM-E4AF.

</details>


### [33] [EdgeCompress: Coupling Multidimensional Model Compression and Dynamic Inference for EdgeAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06982)
*Hao Kong,Di Liu,Shuo Huai,Xiangzhong Luo,Ravi Subramaniam,Christian Makaya,Qian Lin,Weichen Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EdgeCompress proposes a multidimensional CNN compression framework combining dynamic input cropping (DIC) and compound shrinking (CS) with a dynamic cascaded inference strategy to reduce computation on embedded devices while improving accuracy on ImageNet-1K (ResNet-50: -48.8% FLOPs, +0.8% top-1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable deployment of high-performance CNNs on resource-constrained hardware by reducing redundant computation both at the input level and within the network architecture, without sacrificing accuracy.

Method: 1) Dynamic image cropping (DIC) with a lightweight foreground predictor to crop informative foreground regions; 2) Compound shrinking (CS) to jointly reduce depth, width, and resolution of networks based on accuracy/computation trade-offs; 3) Dynamic inference framework with cascaded models of varying complexity chosen per input instance to maximize efficiency.

Result: On ImageNet-1K, EdgeCompress reduces ResNet-50 FLOPs by 48.8% and increases top-1 accuracy by 0.8%; compared to HRank, accuracy improves by 4.1% at similar computation levels.

Conclusion: EdgeCompress effectively reduces computational redundancy in both data and models, enabling more efficient deployment of advanced CNNs on embedded hardware and offering a scalable framework for adaptive inference.

Abstract: Convolutional neural networks (CNNs) have demonstrated encouraging results in image classification tasks. However, the prohibitive computational cost of CNNs hinders the deployment of CNNs onto resource-constrained embedded devices. To address this issue, we propose EdgeCompress, a comprehensive compression framework to reduce the computational overhead of CNNs. In EdgeCompress, we first introduce dynamic image cropping (DIC), where we design a lightweight foreground predictor to accurately crop the most informative foreground object of input images for inference, which avoids redundant computation on background regions. Subsequently, we present compound shrinking (CS) to collaboratively compress the three dimensions (depth, width, and resolution) of CNNs according to their contribution to accuracy and model computation. DIC and CS together constitute a multidimensional CNN compression framework, which is able to comprehensively reduce the computational redundancy in both input images and neural network architectures, thereby improving the inference efficiency of CNNs. Further, we present a dynamic inference framework to efficiently process input images with different recognition difficulties, where we cascade multiple models with different complexities from our compression framework and dynamically adopt different models for different input images, which further compresses the computational redundancy and improves the inference efficiency of CNNs, facilitating the deployment of advanced CNNs onto embedded hardware. Experiments on ImageNet-1K demonstrate that EdgeCompress reduces the computation of ResNet-50 by 48.8% while improving the top-1 accuracy by 0.8%. Meanwhile, we improve the accuracy by 4.1% with similar computation compared to HRank, the state-of-the-art compression framework. The source code and models are available at https://github.com/ntuliuteam/edge-compress

</details>


### [34] [Ego-Human Motion Prediction with 3D-Aware LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07001)
*Yujin Bae,Jaewoo Jeong,Hyeonseong Kim,Kuk-Jin Yoon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A unified egocentric motion forecasting model, Ego3DLM, predicts past/future poses and past/future narration in one autoregressive pass, grounding in 3D scene context and language, achieving state-of-the-art on Nymeria.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Egocentric forecasting is highly ill-posed and current methods either separate pose and language predictions or neglect explicit 3D spatial/semantic grounding. Integrating 3D scene understanding with cross-modal, single-pass inference should yield more plausible and semantically coherent motion forecasts.

Method: Three-stage training: (1) spatial-semantic scene awareness pretraining; (2) holistic instruction tuning over past/future poses and narrations in a single autoregressive pass; (3) GRPO-based reinforcement finetuning with intra- and inter-modal rewards to optimize pose-language fidelity. Architecture takes three-point tracking, 3D scene features, and egocentric video to decode past pose, future pose, past narration, and future narration in one pass, enforcing cross-modal and temporal consistency.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance across future motion prediction, past motion tracking, and motion description on the Nymeria benchmark, showing that 3D grounding and holistic cross-modal prediction yield physically plausible and semantically coherent forecasts.

Conclusion: Ego3DLM demonstrates the value of grounding egocentric forecasting in 3D scene semantics and predicting poses and language jointly in a single pass, providing a unified framework and training recipe for integrated motion understanding.

Abstract: Anticipating human motion from an egocentric perspective is fundamental for proactive assistance in AR/VR, human-robot collaboration, and embodied AI. While recent works incorporate language as a semantic prior to reduce the ill-posed nature of egocentric forecasting, they largely neglect the 3D spatial and semantic context that governs how motion unfolds, and treat pose and language prediction as separate inference streams. We introduce Ego3DLM, built on two core principles: accurate motion forecasting requires explicit spatial and semantic understanding of the 3D environment, and pose and language must be predicted holistically in a single pass, since motion is inherently tied to the semantic interpretation of actions being performed. Given three-point tracking, 3D scene features, and egocentric video, Ego3DLM simultaneously decodes past pose, future pose, past narration, and future narration in a single autoregressive pass, grounding predicted poses and descriptions in one another to enforce cross-modal and temporal consistency. We adopt a three-stage training scheme: (1) spatial-semantic scene awareness pretraining; (2) holistic instruction tuning over all four outputs in a single pass; and (3) GRPO-based reinforcement finetuning with intra- and inter-modal rewards that directly optimize pose-language fidelity. Experiments on the Nymeria benchmark demonstrate that Ego3DLM achieves state-of-the-art performance across future motion prediction, past motion tracking, and motion description, showing that 3D scene grounding and holistic cross-modal prediction yield physically plausible and semantically coherent motion forecasts. The project page is available at https://jaewoo97.github.io/Ego3DLM/.

</details>


### [35] [SHTA: Semantic Hard Token Correction and Center Alignment for Semi-Supervised Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07019)
*Zhuoru Zhang,Yiheng Zhong,Zimu Zhang,Xiaofeng Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SHTA, a lightweight training-time semantic representation branch that improves semantic consistency in hard regions for semi-supervised medical image segmentation by Semantic Assignment, Hard Token Refinement, and Semantic Center Alignment, without extra prediction supervision or inference cost, yielding consistent improvements when integrated into GA-CPS, CPS, URPC, and MagicNet on Synapse and AMOS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current semi-supervised methods enhance supervision quality but do not explicitly enforce semantic consistency of learned representations in hard regions. As prediction-level supervision strengthens, hard regions with unstable semantic assignment may not develop consistent representations, limiting further gains. A training-time semantic-consistency mechanism is needed, with minimal impact on inference.

Method: Proposes SHTA (Semantic Hard Token Correction and Center Alignment), a lightweight training-time semantic branch consisting of: (1) Semantic Assignment to refine how hard-region features map to semantic tokens, (2) Hard Token Refinement to adjust representations of difficult tokens, (3) Semantic Center Alignment to align semantic centers with token representations. It integrates into existing semi-supervised segmentation frameworks (GA-CPS, CPS, URPC, MagicNet) without adding prediction-level supervision or inference cost, preserving the original prediction pathway.

Result: Evaluations on Synapse and AMOS show consistent improvements across the integrated frameworks, particularly in segmentation accuracy, weak-organ recovery, and semantic ambiguity reduction, with improvements paired with only training-time overhead and no extra inference cost.

Conclusion: SHTA effectively enforces semantic consistency in hard regions during training, yielding improved segmentation performance across multiple semi-supervised frameworks while maintaining the original inference pathway and incurring minimal overhead; it demonstrates strong compatibility and practical utility for semi-supervised medical image segmentation.

Abstract: Recent advances in semi-supervised medical image segmentation have achieved remarkable performance through prediction consistency, pseudo-label supervision, and hard-region supervision. However, these methods primarily improve supervision quality rather than explicitly enforcing semantic consistency in the learned representations of hard regions. Consequently, even under increasingly stronger prediction-level supervision, difficult regions exhibiting unstable semantic assignment often fail to establish semantically consistent representations during training, thereby limiting further segmentation improvement. To address this issue, we propose SHTA (Semantic Hard Token Correction and Center Alignment), a lightweight training-time semantic representation branch. Instead of introducing additional prediction supervision, SHTA refines intermediate semantic representations through Semantic Assignment, Hard Token Refinement, and Semantic Center Alignment, thereby improving semantic consistency in hard regions while preserving the original prediction pathway and introducing no additional inference cost. We integrate SHTA into representative semi-supervised segmentation frameworks, including GA-CPS, CPS, URPC, and MagicNet, and conduct evaluations on the Synapse and AMOS datasets. Experimental results demonstrate that SHTA delivers consistent paired improvements across frameworks, with especially clear gains in segmentation accuracy, weak-organ recovery, and semantic ambiguity reduction, while incurring only training-time overhead. The code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/release_SHTA-42D5/.

</details>


### [36] [AnchorPrune: Relevance-Anchored Contextual Expansion for Visual Token Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07033)
*Kyuan Oh,Bumsoo Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AnchorPrune is a training-free token pruning method for vision-language models that first selects a relevance anchor and then expands with novelty-aware context to maintain query-critical evidence while improving efficiency under severe token budgets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large vision-language models incur high inference costs with high-resolution inputs producing thousands of tokens; existing pruning methods trade off relevance and diversity and can fail under aggressive compression.

Method: Build a protected relevance anchor from relevance-ranked tokens; determine anchor size from novelty profile; allocate remaining budget via importance-weighted novelty to add informative, non-redundant context relative to the anchor; ordered design prevents expansion from displacing query cues; no retraining or model modification; architecture-aware and compatible across image and video VLMs.

Result: Consistently improves accuracy-efficiency trade-offs over training-free baselines, especially under severe compression; achieves high token efficiency (e.g., 97.6% of full-token performance with 160/2880 tokens on LLaVA-NeXT-7B); effective across benchmarks and modalities.

Conclusion: Relevance-anchored contextual expansion is an effective principle for efficient multimodal inference; AnchorPrune is lightweight, training-free, and broadly applicable, with code available.

Abstract: Large vision-language models incur substantial inference costs because high-resolution inputs introduce thousands of visual tokens, many of which are redundant for a given query. Existing pruning methods often combine query relevance and token diversity, yet these objectives can conflict under aggressive compression: relevance-driven selection may overconcentrate the budget on correlated local evidence, while diversity-driven selection may suppress indispensable tokens or retain distinct but uninformative regions. We introduce AnchorPrune, a training-free framework that first constructs a protected relevance anchor and then expands it with complementary visual context. AnchorPrune adaptively determines the anchor size from the novelty profile of relevance-ranked tokens, preserving a compact set of query-critical evidence, and allocates the remaining budget through importance-weighted novelty to recover informative, non-redundant context relative to the anchor. This ordered design prevents contextual expansion from displacing indispensable query cues while improving overall visual coverage. AnchorPrune is lightweight, architecture-aware, and requires neither retraining nor model modification. Across image and video vision-language models and benchmarks, it consistently improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off over training-free baselines, particularly under severe compression. On LLaVA-NeXT-7B, AnchorPrune preserves 97.6% of full-token performance using only 160 of 2,880 visual tokens. These results establish relevance-anchored contextual expansion as an effective principle for efficient multimodal inference. Code is available at https://github.com/MULTI-cau/AnchorPrune.

</details>


### [37] [TRACE-Seg3D: Counterfactual Context Auditing For Robust 3D Glioma Segmentation Under Institutional Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07038)
*Nguyen Linh Dan Le,Nguyen Pham Hoang Le,Tran Dang Khoi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TRACE-Seg3D introduces counterfactual context auditing for 3D medical image segmentation to assess robustness under distribution shifts by pairing segmentation with audit evidence and systematically varying imaging context, revealing context-sensitive failures beyond Dice/HD95.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical segmentation models perform well in-distribution but exploit nuisance cues when context shifts (scanner/protocol/institution) occur, compromising reliability; existing metrics miss these failures.

Method: Propose TRACE-Seg3D framework that preserves lesion evidence while counterfactually varying imaging context; provides audit evidence for context sensitivity and anatomical plausibility; enables case-level reliability assessment; tested on 3D segmentation benchmarks with cross-domain evaluation.

Result: Competitive in-distribution and cross-domain performance on BraTS and UTSW glioma segmentation; TRACE-Seg3D identifies context-sensitive failure modes not captured by Dice or HD95.

Conclusion: Counterfactual context auditing offers a practical path to transparent, reliable 3D medical image segmentation under distribution shift; code available.

Abstract: Medical image segmentation models can achieve strong benchmark performance while remaining sensitive to scanner, protocol, and institutional variation. These context shifts alter image appearance without changing the underlying lesion, allowing models to exploit nuisance cues that Dice and HD95 fail to expose. We present TRACE-Seg3D, a counterfactual context auditing framework for robust 3D medical image segmentation. TRACE-Seg3D preserves lesion-relevant evidence and systematically varies imaging context to quantify prediction stability under controlled context shifts. The framework pairs each segmentation with audit evidence for context sensitivity and anatomical plausibility, enabling case-level reliability assessment beyond overlap-based evaluation. Experiments on BraTS and UTSW glioma segmentation benchmarks demonstrate competitive in-distribution and cross-domain performance. TRACE-Seg3D also exposes context-sensitive failure modes missed by conventional metrics. These results establish counterfactual context auditing as a practical route toward transparent and reliable 3D medical image segmentation under distribution shift. Our code is available at https://github.com/danleneurocom/Counterfactual-Representation-Network.

</details>


### [38] [Making Implicit Preservation Intent Explicit in Conversational Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07051)
*Soomin Han,Jihyung Ahn,Bumsoo Kim,Buru Chang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A diagnostic benchmark (OCCUR-Bench) for temporal preservation in conversational image editing and a training-free framework (ReSpec) that grounds preservation in editing history by using restoration-aware instructions and historical references to faithfully restore occluded-but-unchanged content.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing conversational image editing systems struggle to recover content that temporarily disappears or becomes occluded across turns, often hallucinating or inconsistent results. There is a need to evaluate and enable faithful restoration grounded in editing history rather than relying solely on the current image.

Method: OCCUR-Bench provides diverse occlusion-and-revelation scenarios with historical restoration references to evaluate faithful restoration. ReSpec is training-free: it identifies what should persist from the editing history, selects a historical image state that provides missing visual evidence, and conditions an in-context editor on the instruction and reference image to achieve restoration-aware editing.

Result: Empirical results show ReSpec improves restoration fidelity and temporal consistency on OCCUR-Bench, supporting the claim that grounding preservation in editing history improves outcomes relative to baseline plausible-regeneration approaches.

Conclusion: The work introduces a valuable diagnostic benchmark for temporal preservation in conversational image editing and a practical, training-free method (ReSpec) that leverages historical references to enforce faithful restoration, highlighting the importance of history-grounded preservation.

Abstract: Conversational image editing requires preserving not only visible content, but also content that temporarily disappears across turns. When newly added or modified content occludes a previously visible region, that region should reappear if it was never semantically changed. However, existing systems often fail to recover such occluded-but-unchanged content, producing inconsistent or hallucinated results. We introduce OCCUR-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark for temporal preservation in conversational image editing. OCCUR-Bench provides diverse occlusion-and-revelation scenarios with historical restoration references, enabling evaluation of faithful restoration rather than plausible regeneration. We also propose ReSpec, a training-free framework that makes implicit preservation explicit by pairing restoration-aware instructions with historical visual references. Given an editing history, ReSpec identifies what should persist, selects the historical image state that provides missing visual evidence, and conditions an in-context editor on the resulting instruction and reference image. Experiments show that ReSpec improves restoration fidelity and temporal consistency on OCCUR-Bench, highlighting the need to ground preservation in editing history rather than only the current image.

</details>


### [39] [Navigating Hierarchy: Hyperbolic Learning on Brain Graphs for Disorder Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07077)
*Yapeng Li,Bo Jiang,Ziyan Zhang,Dongdong Chen,Zhengzheng Tu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Hyperbolic learning framework HLBG models hierarchical brain networks (ROIs, communities, whole brain) using Lorentzian hyperbolic space and geometric entailment constraints; introduces GaMamba to capture long-range dependencies with structural prompts; reported state-of-the-art on ABIDE-I and REST-MDD and potential biomarkers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Brain networks exhibit hierarchical organization across multiple levels (ROI, community, whole-brain). Existing graph models struggle to capture ROI-community interactions and hierarchy; a hyperbolic geometry approach could naturally encode hierarchical relationships to improve discrimination and biomarker discovery.

Method: Project ROI, community, and whole-brain representations into Lorentzian hyperbolic space. Enforce hierarchy with two geometric entailment constraints. Develop Graph-aware Mamba (GaMamba) to integrate topology-derived prompts into Mamba for long-range dependencies while preserving graph topology. Evaluate on ABIDE-I and REST-MDD.

Result: HLBG outperforms state-of-the-art methods on the tested datasets and identifies disorder-relevant functional biomarkers.

Conclusion: Hyperbolic geometry provides an effective framework for capturing hierarchical brain network structure across multiple levels, with HLBG achieving improved performance and interpretable biomarkers; GaMamba enhances long-range interaction modeling within this framework.

Abstract: Functional brain networks exhibit a hierarchical organization across ROI, community, and whole-brain levels, supporting local processing, inter-community coordination, and global integration. Recent studies have demonstrated that brain community-aware modeling is beneficial for both diagnosis and biomarker identification of brain networks. However, existing brain graph modeling methods often struggle to model ROI-community interactions, thereby failing to fully exploit the hierarchy across ROI, community, and whole-brain network levels. To address this issue, inspired by deep hyperbolic learning in modeling hierarchical structures, we propose a novel framework, termed Hyperbolic Learning on Brain Graphs (HLBG), for brain network analysis. The core idea of HLBG is to exploit the inherent hierarchical geometry of hyperbolic space to model the hierarchical relationships among ROIs, functional communities, and the whole-brain network, thereby learning hierarchy-aware and highly discriminative representations for brain network data. Specifically, HLBG first projects representations from ROIs, communities, and the whole-brain network into Lorentzian hyperbolic space. Then, the multi-level hierarchy is imposed via two geometric entailment constraints. In addition, we introduce a new Graph-aware Mamba (GaMamba) model, which incorporates topology-derived structural prompts into Mamba to capture long-range dependencies while preserving graph topological information. Experiments on ABIDE-I and REST-MDD demonstrate that HLBG outperforms state-of-the-art methods and identifies disorder-relevant functional biomarkers.

</details>


### [40] [AT-Attn: Temporal-Aware Cross-Attention for Longitudinal Multimodal Alzheimer's Disease Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07091)
*Xinyue Du,Yibo Liu,Zhenglei Zhou,Xuancheng Yao,Weimin Zhong,Qiuhui Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Temporal-aware multimodal fusion AT-Attn improves longitudinal AD diagnosis by integrating MRI with clinical trajectories using Change-and-Time encoding, time-biased cross-attention, and gated fusion; evaluated on ADNI with 1,520 patients; achieves competitively high accuracy and AUC, outperforming unimodal and naive fusion baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Irregular visit cadence in longitudinal AD assessment; naive fusion of MRI and clinical data can degrade performance when MRI is noisy/unavailable; need temporal-aware, constrained fusion to leverage longitudinal signals.

Method: Introduce AT-Attn: Change-and-Time encoding; time-biased asymmetric cross-attention; gated fusion; uses MRI (retained) plus six cognitive-scale trajectories and seven static clinical variables; patient-level 5-fold CV; evaluation against unimodal and baselines.

Result: Accuracy 0.719±0.024; macro F1 0.721±0.023; ROC-AUC 0.873±0.013; PR-AUC 0.783±0.018; outperforms unimodal and naive multimodal baselines; competitive with strong tabular baselines.

Conclusion: Temporal-aware constrained fusion enables MRI to provide helpful complementary information for AD diagnosis; demonstrates effectiveness of AT-Attn in multimodal longitudinal data integration.

Abstract: In longitudinal Alzheimer's disease (AD) diagnosis support, clinical and imaging information is often collected at irregular visits. Integrating these multimodal observations may improve diagnostic assessment, but naive fusion can degrade performance when MRI is noisy or intermittently unavailable. We propose AT-Attn, a temporal-aware multimodal framework that combines Change-and-Time encoding, time-biased asymmetric cross-attention, and gated fusion to integrate MRI with longitudinal clinical information. We evaluate AT-Attn on an MRI-retained ADNI cohort of 1,520 patients using structural MRI, six cognitive-scale trajectories, and seven static clinical variables under patient-level five-fold cross-validation. The main asymmetric AT-Attn model achieves accuracy 0.719+/-0.024, macro F1 0.721+/-0.023, ROC-AUC 0.873+/-0.013, and PR-AUC 0.783+/-0.018, outperforming unimodal and naive multimodal fusion baselines while remaining competitive with strong tabular baselines. These results suggest that a temporal-aware and constrained fusion strategy can help structural MRI contribute clinically relevant complementary information for patient-level AD diagnosis support.

</details>


### [41] [Video-Based Detection of squint and cataract for accessibility-aware adaptive web interface rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07099)
*Amar Ranjan Dash,Manas Ranjan Patra*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A real-time video-based system detects squint and estimates cataract presence/severity using MediaPipe Face Mesh landmarks and image analysis, achieving high accuracy and enabling scalable deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the need for low-cost, scalable ocular screening and accessibility-aware interfaces for people with visual impairment.

Method: Record video with standard devices; extract 478-point facial landmarks via MediaPipe Face Mesh to derive ocular features for multi-class squint classification; estimate cataract via grayscale intensity and histogram-based lens opacity analysis; potential integration with adaptive UI and web accessibility.

Result: Squint detection accuracy 98.39%; cataract presence/severity accuracy 96.90%.

Conclusion: Demonstrates a viable, low-cost framework for concurrent squint and cataract screening with potential for large-scale deployment and integration with visual impairment support systems.

Abstract: Squint and cataract are major ocular disorders that majorly affect visual perception and interaction capability. This paper proposes a real-time video-based automated detection system for squint and cataract detection based on computer vision and image processing methods. The proposed system uses a media-pipe face-mesh (a 478-point facial landmark detection model) to extract geometric ocular features for multi-class squint classification. Simultaneously, The presence and severity cataract is estimated through grayscale intensity and histogram-based lens opacity analysis. The system records short video sequences with standard laptop or mobile cameras, which can be deployed at low costs and on a large scale. The experimental performance has shown great accuracy in the detection of squint (98.39%) and classification of cataract (96.90%). Besides automatic ocular analysis, the proposed framework is also made accessible for visual impairment inference which will be integrated with future adaptive user interface and Web accessibility systems for people with visual impairment.

</details>


### [42] [Tree-of-Thoughts Reasoning for Text-to-Image In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07117)
*Stepanida Alekseeva,Jenifer Kalafatovich,Seong-Whan Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces a Tree-of-Thoughts (ToT) framework for Text-to-Image in-context learning (T2I-ICL) that uses multi-branch reasoning to improve prompt construction and image generation without additional training, validated on CoBSAT and outperforming baselines and Chain-of-Thought prompting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: State-of-the-art multimodal LLMs struggle with T2I-ICL due to limited compositional reasoning and sensitivity to prompt construction, causing difficulty in inferring latent patterns from few-shot demonstrations.

Method: A multi-stage ToT reasoning framework with a generation–evaluation–selection layer that produces multiple candidate hypotheses, evaluates and selects a coherent interpretation, and then constructs the final prompt for image synthesis. Implemented in a complete ToT-T2IICL inference pipeline that explores alternative reasoning branches to mitigate ambiguity and compositional errors. No additional training or fine-tuning required.

Result: Qualitative and quantitative results on the CoBSAT benchmark show that structured multi-branch ToT reasoning yields more consistent and semantically aligned image generation than baseline and Chain-of-Thought prompting methods.

Conclusion: Structured multi-branch ToT prompts improve T2I-ICL performance by reducing ambiguity and compositional errors, offering a plug-and-play improvement for image synthesis with existing models.

Abstract: In text-to-image in-context learning (T2I-ICL), a model has to infer a latent compositional pattern from fewshot demonstrations for generating a query image. Recent studies show that state-of-the-art multimodal large language models struggle with this setting, particularly due to limited compositional reasoning and sensitivity to prompt construction. In this work, we propose a Tree-of-Thoughts (ToT) reasoning framework for T2I-ICL that introduces a multi-stage reasoning and selection layer that generates, evaluates, and selects among multiple candidate hypotheses before constructing the final prompt for image synthesis. By exploring alternative reasoning branches and selecting a coherent interpretation, the proposed approach mitigates prompt ambiguity and compositional errors. We implement the proposed approach in a complete ToT-T2IICL inference pipeline and evaluate it on the CoBSAT benchmark. Both qualitative and quantitative results show that structured multi-branch reasoning leads to more consistent and semantically aligned image generation compared to baseline and Chain-of-Thought prompting strategies, without any additional training or fine-tuning.

</details>


### [43] [ColorFM: An Optimization-to-Learning Framework for Color Transfer via Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07119)
*Yuhang He,Kai Zhang,Xiaoming Li,Du Chen,Jian Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Optimization-to-learning framework for color transfer that treats color mapping as transport of pixel distributions along velocity fields via Flow Matching, with an optimization-based ColorFM-O and a fast feed-forward ColorFM-L that learns from generated pseudo-supervised pairs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current color transfer methods struggle with global mapping inaccuracies, semantic misalignment, and artifacts. A flow-based distribution transport offers a semantically aware, precise transfer while enabling data generation for training a fast model.

Method: Formulates color transfer as transport of pixel distributions along velocity fields using Flow Matching. ColorFM-O performs instance-specific optimization by fitting a velocity field through hierarchical color coupling guided by semantic priors; integrates flow to produce transfer and generate pseudo-supervised data. ColorFM-L trains a feed-forward model with implicit state modeling to predict flow parameters for bidirectional linearized transport, enabling fast, accurate color transfer.

Result: ColorFM-L achieves state-of-the-art visual quality, structural fidelity, and semantic consistency, combining the accuracy of optimization with the speed of inference.

Conclusion: The proposed ColorFM framework effectively bridges optimization and learning for semantically aware color transfer, delivering high-quality results with efficient inference.

Abstract: Color transfer aims to align the color distribution of a source image with that of a reference image while preserving structural and semantic consistency. However, existing methods often suffer from inaccurate global mapping, semantic misalignment, and visual artifacts. To address these issues, we propose ColorFM, an optimization-to-learning framework. ColorFM connects online optimization to offline inference by reformulating color transfer as the transport of pixel distributions along velocity fields via Flow Matching. Specifically, we introduce ColorFM-O, an instance-specific optimization scheme that fits the velocity field through hierarchical color coupling guided by semantic priors. By numerically integrating the induced flow trajectories, ColorFM-O produces precise and semantically consistent color transfer results, while generating high-quality paired data as pseudo-supervision. Building upon this, we design ColorFM-L, an efficient feed-forward model trained on the generated pairs. Through implicit state modeling, ColorFM-L extracts deep semantic features to predict flow parameters for bidirectional linearized transport, ensuring accurate color transfer. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ColorFM-L outperforms state-of-the-art methods in visual quality, structural fidelity, and semantic consistency, successfully combining the accuracy of optimization with the speed of feed-forward inference.

</details>


### [44] [Widest-Path Reachability Fields for Connectivity-Preserving Slender Structure Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07123)
*Youcheng Zong,Runda Jia,Minxuan Hu,Weilan Su,Dakuo He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel differentiable topology-aware loss (WPRF) to counter topological gradient starvation in slender-structure segmentation, routing gradients to connectivity bottlenecks via a Max-Min reachability objective. Introduces OMVIS dataset and shows widespread gains across architectures and datasets (clDice +7.2pp on fragile cases).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pixel-wise losses misallocate gradients for connectivity-critical pixels (bottlenecks), causing topological breaks in slender structures; need a differentiable objective that emphasizes reachability and bottlenecks.

Method: Propose Widest-Path Reachability Fields (WPRF), a differentiable Max-Min objective implemented via dynamic programming on a domain-restricted graph, with a bottleneck-aware observation term. Plug-and-play, backbone-agnostic, no inference overhead. Avoids post-hoc skeletonization/homology by optimizing end-to-end reachability.

Result: WPRF directed gradients toward bottlenecks improves topology; 87% of experiments with fixed hyperparameters show gains; clDice gains of 7.2 percentage points on structurally fragile datasets. Validated across 9 architectures and 6 datasets, with introduction of OMVIS dataset.

Conclusion: Efficient, end-to-end, differentiable reachability optimization concentrates learning on connectivity bottlenecks, outperforming topology-aware losses that rely on skeletonization or homology; WPRF offers practical, generalizable gains without extra inference cost.

Abstract: Segmenting slender curvilinear structures such as retinal vessels, cracks, and roads demands topological correctness, as even a single-pixel discontinuity can fragment a continuous network and invalidate downstream analysis. Under standard binary-mask supervision, models optimized for pixel-level overlap frequently produce topologically broken predictions. We trace this to a fundamental mismatch: pixel-wise losses distribute gradients uniformly, yet connectivity hinges on a sparse set of bottleneck pixels. These pixels are vastly outnumbered by thick structures and background, rendering their aggregate gradient contribution negligible. We term this phenomenon topological gradient starvation (TGS). To address it, we propose Widest-Path Reachability Fields (WPRF), a differentiable Max-Min reachability objective that redirects gradient flow to connectivity bottlenecks. The module is plug-and-play, backbone-agnostic, and incurs no inference overhead. WPRF implements a differentiable Max-Min objective via dynamic programming on a domain-restricted graph, coupled with a bottleneck-aware observation term that balances gradient contributions across varying structures. Compared to prior topology-aware losses that rely on post-hoc skeletonization or homology computation, WPRF directly optimizes end-to-end reachability via differentiable Max-Min algebra, enabling gradient flow to concentrate on connectivity bottlenecks without auxiliary structures. We introduce OMVIS, a new oral microvessel segmentation dataset. Experiments across nine architectures and six datasets validate the bottleneck-focused gradient routing mechanism. WPRF improves 87\% of experiments with fixed hyperparameters and achieves clDice gains of 7.2 percentage points on structurally fragile datasets.

</details>


### [45] [Sparse Attention for Dense Open-Vocabulary Prediction in CLIP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07135)
*Fatimah Zohra,Chen Zhao,Shuming Liu,Bernard Ghanem*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Sparse attention in CLIP via alpha-entmax at inference time improves open-vocabulary dense prediction by reducing noise from low-salience tokens.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dense softmax attention in CLIP distributes probability mass to all token pairs, including irrelevant ones, hindering fine-grained localization in open-vocabulary tasks.

Method: Replace row-wise softmax with alpha-entmax in the final visual self-attention layers, applied to both standard query-key attention and self-correlation variants; entmax yields a data-dependent threshold mapping low scores to zero, redistributing mass to salient tokens.

Result: On open-vocabulary tasks (dense semantic segmentation: Pascal VOC, Pascal Context, ADE20K; FG-OVD retrieval), sparsified attention yields gains that scale with how much the baseline attention spread off the target class.

Conclusion: Inference-time attention sparsification with entmax denoises context and improves dense/open-vocabulary prediction, offering a simple, effective refinement for CLIP-based architectures.

Abstract: Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) relies on softmax-based self-attention, a strictly positive distribution that assigns probability mass to every pair of tokens-even semantically irrelevant ones. While these dense softmax weights are effective for gathering broad context during pre-training, they spread attention across many low-salience tokens, producing noise that obscures the fine-grained, spatially localized cues required for dense, open-vocabulary prediction. We study an inference-time substitution of the row-wise softmax in the final visual self-attention layers with the $α$-entmax transform, applied across both the standard query-key attention and self-correlation variants. Because entmax applies a data-dependent threshold that maps low scores exactly to zero, it acts as an implicit denoiser, zeroing contextually irrelevant dependencies while redistributing mass onto the most relevant tokens. We evaluate on open-vocabulary tasks-dense semantic segmentation (Pascal VOC, Pascal Context, ADE20K) and fine-grained retrieval (FG-OVD)-and find the gain from attention sparsification is proportional to how much the baseline attention spreads off the target class.

</details>


### [46] [ASFR-Net: Adversarial Alignment and Spatio-Frequency Refinement Network for Heterogeneous Remote Sensing Image Change Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07161)
*Xin-Jie Wu,Zhi-Hui You,Si-Bao Chen,Qing-Ling Shu,Xiao Wang,Jin Tang,Bin Luo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: End-to-end ASFR-Net for heterogeneous change detection that decouples genuine land-cover changes from modality differences via MIR-Learner, SFEM, and HGFM; introduces VisNIR-HCD dataset; achieves state-of-the-art results on VisNIR-HCD and public datasets; code available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heterogeneous change detection is hampered by significant modal disparities between imaging modalities, causing pseudo-changes and degraded accuracy. There is a need for modality-invariant representations, suppression of sensor-specific artifacts, and robust benchmarks for building-change detection under high-resolution conditions.

Method: Propose ASFR-Net with three main components: (1) MIR-Learner to guide backbone toward modality-invariant features; (2) SFEM leveraging frequency-domain processing to identify and suppress sensor-specific noise/artefacts; (3) cascaded HGFM-based decoder to fuse multi-level difference features and produce precise change maps. Additionally, release VisNIR-HCD as a high-resolution, building-change benchmark to address data scarcity in heterogeneous tasks.

Result: Extensive experiments on VisNIR-HCD and public datasets show ASFR-Net achieves state-of-the-art performance, significantly outperforming existing methods and demonstrating strong generalization to heterogeneous tasks.

Conclusion: ASFR-Net effectively decouples true changes from modality-induced discrepancies using a spatio-frequency refinement strategy and modality-invariant learning, backed by a high-resolution benchmark. The work provides a robust framework for heterogeneous HCD and a valuable dataset, with code and data publicly available.

Abstract: The core challenge of heterogeneous change detection in remote sensing imagery lies in effectively decoupling genuine land-cover changes from significant modal disparities caused by distinct imaging mechanisms. These intrinsic inconsistencies are prone to introducing pseudo-changes, thereby constraining detection accuracy. To address this, we propose a novel, end-to-end adversarial spatio-frequency refinement network (ASFR-Net). Initially, a modality-invariant representation learner (MIR-Learner) guides the backbone to extract modality-invariant features, effectively bridging the primary domain gap. Subsequently, to address persistent residual modal differences, we design an innovative spatio-frequency synergistic enhancement module (SFEM), which identifies and suppresses sensor-specific noise and artifacts that are difficult to discern in the spatial domain by leveraging frequency-domain processing. Multi-level difference features are then computed from these refined representations and fed into a decoder equipped with cascaded hierarchical guided fusion module (HGFM) blocks to generate precise change maps. To alleviate the data scarcity in heterogeneous tasks, we construct and release a new high-resolution benchmark specifically focused on building changes: the visible-near-infrared heterogeneous change detection (VisNIR-HCD) dataset. It presents unique scientific challenges arising from deceptive visual similarity and non-linear spectral inversions, providing a robust platform for evaluating model generalization. Extensive experiments on VisNIR-HCD and public datasets demonstrate that ASFR-Net achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance, significantly outperforming existing methods. The source code and the VisNIR-HCD dataset are publicly available at https://github.com/LuoYang2024/ASFR-Net.

</details>


### [47] [NoDrift3R: Raymap-Guided Coupling for Drift-Robust Unposed Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07168)
*Xiangyu Sun,Liu Liu,Seungkwon Yang,Jingbing Han,Seungtae Nam,Zhizhong Su,Eunbyung Park*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A drift-robust pose-free 3D Gaussian splatting framework that couples geometry and appearance via a Raymap-Guided Coupling Module and a Dual-Frequency Viewpoint Scheduling to stabilize long-sequence learning, improving rendering quality and camera pose estimation without external pose supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pose drift in long image sequences degrades 3DGS reconstruction. SfM-derived pseudo poses are noisy, and pure rendering-based supervision leads to optimization instability due to geometry-pose entanglement. A unified method is needed to stabilize learning while preserving drift robustness.

Method: Introduce Raymap-Guided Coupling (RGC) that anchors Gaussian centers to raymap-derived geometry; jointly optimize RGB reconstruction, raymap consistency, and camera regularization under a unified objective, creating a bidirectional feedback loop where geometry improves rendering and appearance supervision refines geometry and pose. Employ Dual-Frequency Viewpoint Scheduling: easy-to-hard interval expansion plus replay of short-interval pairs to stabilize learning across long temporal ranges.

Result: Across in-domain and cross-domain datasets, the approach yields consistent improvements in both rendering fidelity and pose estimation, with notably enhanced robustness on long sequences. Ablations confirm that explicit geometry-appearance synergy is key to scalable and drift-robust pose-free feed-forward 3D reconstruction.

Conclusion: The geometry-appearance synergy, reinforced by Raymap-Guided coupling and dual-frequency viewpoint scheduling, enables drift-robust pose-free 3DGS on long sequences, mitigating pose drift and stabilizing optimization while delivering improved rendering and pose estimation.

Abstract: Pose-Free Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has recently emerged as a powerful paradigm for fast scene reconstruction. However, its performance degrades significantly in long image sequences due to cumulative camera pose estimation drift, which propagates errors into geometric modeling and severely limits rendering fidelity. In this work, we revisit the long-sequence bottleneck and identify pose drift as the primary factor restricting reconstruction quality. Furthermore, while SfM-based pseudo ground-truth poses introduce sensor noise, purely rendering-based supervision often leads to optimization instability and local minima due to the entangled optimization of geometry and pose. To address the challenges, we propose a synergistic pose-free framework that explicitly couples geometry and appearance via a Raymap-Guided Coupling Module (RGC). Concretely, we anchor Gaussian centers to raymap-induced geometry and jointly optimize RGB reconstruction, raymap consistency, and camera regularization under a unified objective, yielding a bidirectional feedback loop: stronger geometry improves rendering, and appearance supervision in turn refines geometry and pose. To further stabilize learning across wide temporal ranges, we introduce a Dual-Frequency Viewpoint Scheduling strategy that combines easy-to-hard interval expansion with replay of short-interval pairs. Extensive experiments across in-domain and cross-domain datasets show consistent gains in both rendering and pose estimation, with notably improved robustness on long sequences. Ablation studies validate our central insight: explicitly designed geometry-appearance synergy is the key to scalable and drift-robust pose-free feed-forward 3D reconstruction.

</details>


### [48] [TACoS: Weakly Supervised Learning of Two-Dimensional Materials from Scribble Annotations to Precise Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07169)
*Jiabei Chen,Liping Zhang,Jiang-Bin Wu,Zhongming Wei,Enhao Ning,Su Yan,Weijun Li,Ping-Heng Tan,Xin Ning*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TACoS is a semi-supervised scribble segmentation framework for 2D materials that combines cosine-based consistency, tree-energy regularization, and asymmetric regional contrast learning to reach near fully-supervised performance with extremely few annotations on graphene and MoS2 datasets; robust to weak edges and complex backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dense pixel-wise annotations for 2D material flake segmentation are costly and time-consuming, hindering deployment of segmentation models in high-throughput screening.

Method: A unified framework that integrates semi-supervised consistency learning with structured tree energy constraints. Key components: (1) unlabeled weak-strong distribution alignment via cosine consistency across views, (2) tree energy regularization using minimum spanning trees to derive pixel affinities and generate structure-aware soft pseudo labels. Additionally, asymmetric regional contrast learning fuses high-confidence predictions with scribbles to form augmented labels, constructs category prototypes, and imposes contrastive constraints on boundary-unlabeled pixels to improve intra-class cohesion and inter-class separation.

Result: On graphene and MoS2 datasets, TACoS achieves over 96% of fully supervised performance using less than 0.6% annotated data, with superior structural coherence and boundary stability in challenging, low-contrast, and complex-background scenarios.

Conclusion: TACoS provides an efficient, scalable solution for automated high-throughput screening of 2D material flakes, delivering near-supervised accuracy with minimal labeling and robust boundary handling.

Abstract: The precise pixel-level localization of 2D material flakes is crucial for high-throughput screening. However, traditional fully supervised methods rely on dense annotations, which are costly and time-consuming, severely limiting the practical deployment of segmentation models. This paper proposes TACoS, a specialized scribble segmentation framework tailored for 2D materials. First, we design a unified framework that integrates semi-supervised consistency learning with structured tree energy constraints. This framework comprises two core components: an unlabeled weak-strong distribution alignment module and a tree energy regularization module. The former employs cosine consistency constraints to enhance prediction alignment across views. Meanwhile, the latter utilizes minimum spanning trees to establish pixel affinity relationships and generate structure-aware soft pseudo labels for online semantic guidance. Next, we introduce asymmetric regional contrast learning. This approach fuses high-confidence predictions from the weak augmentation branch with scribbles to form augmented labels, and construct category prototypes in the representation space. Simultaneously, we prioritize contrastive constraints on challenging pixels in boundary-unlabeled regions. This strategy enhances intra-class cohesion and inter-class separation at the representation level, effectively reducing category confusion in low-contrast edges and complex backgrounds. Experiments conducted on the constructed graphene and MoS2 datasets demonstrate that our method TACoS achieves over 96% of fully supervised performance using less than 0.6% annotated data. Furthermore, it exhibits superior structural coherence and boundary stability in scenarios with weakly contrasting edges and complex backgrounds, providing an efficient and scalable solution for automated high-throughput screening of 2D material flakes.

</details>


### [49] [PUF: Plug-and-Play Uncertainty-Aware Fusion for Online 3D Scene Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07170)
*Yi Yang,Myrna Castillo,Bodo Rosenhahn,Michael Ying Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A probabilistic, uncertainty-aware online 3D scene graph fusion framework (PUF) that replaces deterministic fusion with Dirichlet evidence accumulation to handle observation, 2D model, and 3D representation uncertainties; supports class-conditional priors and works with 3D Gaussian/voxel backends; achieves state-of-the-art on 3DSSG/ReplicaSSG with real-time latency; code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Online 3D scene graph fusion is typically deterministic and ignores three uncertainty sources: observation noise, errors in 2D perception, and ambiguities in 3D representations. To improve accuracy and robustness without sacrificing speed, a probabilistic fusion mechanism is needed.

Method: PUF is plug-and-play and uncertainty-aware; reframes node association as probabilistic likelihood over semantic and spatial factors; uses Dirichlet evidence accumulation to distribute evidence across candidate associations proportional to their likelihood; optionally uses a class-conditional prior to complete edges for rarely co-observed pairs; demonstrated with both 3D Gaussian and 3D voxel backends.

Result: Empirically outperforms existing online fusion methods on 3DSSG and ReplicaSSG benchmarks, while preserving real-time latency; shows generalization across different 3D representations.

Conclusion: Uncertainty-aware fusion provides a principled and effective paradigm for online 3D scene understanding; broader applicability across backends demonstrated; code available at the given URL.

Abstract: Online 3D scene graph generation builds a persistent, structured representation of a scene by incrementally fusing 2D observations into a global 3D graph. Existing online methods treat this fusion as a fully deterministic pipeline, where we identify three sources of uncertainty that are overlooked: observation, 2D model, and 3D representation. We propose PUF: a Plug-and-play, Uncertainty-aware, and training-free Fusion framework. Scene graph node association is reformulated as a probabilistic likelihood over semantic and spatial factors, replacing binary accept/reject gates. Dirichlet evidence accumulation distributes class and relationship evidence across plausible candidates proportional to association likelihood. An optional class-conditional prior completes edges for sparsely or never co-observed object pairs. We instantiate PUF with both a 3D Gaussian and a 3D voxel backend and observe consistent improvements, demonstrating its ability to generalize across different representations. Experiments on the 3DSSG and ReplicaSSG benchmarks show that our method substantially outperforms existing approaches while maintaining real-time latency.
